# 故障排查方法论：CANN日志分析与常见错误代码解读

在昇腾（Ascend）平台上部署和调试DeepSeek 大模型 ，对于习惯了NVIDIA CUDA生态的开发者来说，初次上手往往会经历一段“阵痛期”。当终端屏幕上滚动出现一连串红色的 `EZ9999` 或 `ACL Stream Synchronize Failed` 报错时，那种无力感是真实的。与CUDA相对直观的报错栈不同，CANN（Compute Architecture for Neural Networks ）作为一套复杂的异构计算架构，其错误日志往往涉及到底层的算子编译（ATC）、图执行（GE）、运行时（Runtime）以及硬件任务调度（Task Scheduler）。

然而，恐惧源于未知。CANN的日志系统虽然庞大，但有着严密的逻辑结构。如果你掌握了日志分析的核心方法论，这些晦涩的报错信息其实是通往真相的高清地图。本篇将深入剖析昇腾平台的故障排查体系，从日志结构、错误码解读、调试工具使用到系统化的排查策略，带你走出“玄学调参”的误区。

### [](https://blog.csdn.net/qq_46207024/article/details/157128456)[](https://blog.csdn.net/qq_46207024/article/details/157128456)1. 深入理解CANN日志架构：日志去哪儿了？

首先，我们需要建立“全栈日志”的视角。在昇腾系统中，一个简单的 PyTorch 算子执行，实际上跨越了Host侧（CPU）和Device侧（NPU）两个物理域，以及Framework、CANN Software、Driver、Firmware等多个软件层级。

#### [](https://blog.csdn.net/qq_46207024/article/details/157128456)[](https://blog.csdn.net/qq_46207024/article/details/157128456)1.1 应用层日志（PLOG）：Host侧的第一现场

这是开发者最常打交道的日志，通常被称为 PLOG。它记录了PyTorch/MindSpore框架与CANN接口（ACL）交互的全过程。

- **默认位置**：`~/ascend/log/plog/` 或 `/var/log/npu/slog/host-0/`。
- **关键内容**：
  
  - **算子编译请求**：框架下发的算子名称、Shape、Dtype。
  - **内存申请与释放**：HBM内存池的动态变化。
  - **图构建过程**：GE（Graph Engine）对计算图的优化与拆解。
  - **异常堆栈**：Python层面的报错往往是PLOG中底层错误的直接映射。

当你看到 Python 抛出 `RuntimeError: ACL stream synchronize failed` 时，这只是冰山一角。第一时间应该去 PLOG 目录下，按时间排序找到最新的 `plog-*.log` 文件，搜索 `ERROR` 或 `EVENT` 关键字。

#### [](https://blog.csdn.net/qq_46207024/article/details/157128456)[](https://blog.csdn.net/qq_46207024/article/details/157128456)1.2 设备侧日志（Device Log）：芯片内部的黑匣子

当错误发生在芯片内部（AICore计算溢出、地址越界、L2 Cache校验错误）时，Host侧往往只能收到一个笼统的“执行失败”信号，真正的“案发现场”在Device侧。

- **默认位置**：`/var/log/npu/slog/device-id/`（id对应卡号，如device-0, device-1）。
- **关键内容**：
  
  - **AICore Exception**：具体的算子执行异常，如 `Vector Core Overflow`。
  - **HCCS Link Status**：多卡通信时的链路状态变化。
  - **TS（Task Scheduler）日志**：硬件任务队列的调度情况。

**注意**：Device日志通常是二进制压缩格式或经过编码的，直接阅读较为困难，通常需要结合Host侧日志的时间戳进行关联分析。

#### [](https://blog.csdn.net/qq_46207024/article/details/157128456)[](https://blog.csdn.net/qq_46207024/article/details/157128456)1.3 日志级别控制：不仅是开关

CANN提供了灵活的日志级别控制，通过环境变量 `ASCEND_GLOBAL_LOG_LEVEL` 进行设置：

- `3` (ERROR)：默认级别，仅记录严重错误。生产环境推荐。
- `1` (INFO)：记录关键流程节点。排查逻辑问题时推荐。
- `0` (DEBUG)：记录所有细节，包括每个算子的输入输出描述。**慎用**，这会产生GB级别的日志，严重拖慢运行速度，仅在定位“疑难杂症”时开启。

此外，还可以通过 `ASCEND_SLOG_PRINT_TO_STDOUT=1` 让日志直接输出到终端，省去来回切换文件的麻烦。

### [](https://blog.csdn.net/qq_46207024/article/details/157128456)[](https://blog.csdn.net/qq_46207024/article/details/157128456)2. 错误代码的“摩斯密码”：高频错误解读

CANN的错误码通常以 `E` 开头，后跟一串数字。虽然官方文档有详细索引，但经验告诉我们，80%的故障集中在以下几类。

#### [](https://blog.csdn.net/qq_46207024/article/details/157128456)[](https://blog.csdn.net/qq_46207024/article/details/157128456)2.1 E19999 (Inner Error)：未知的恐惧

这是最让人头疼的“内部错误”，相当于医生告诉你“你有病，但我不知道哪儿疼”。这通常意味着错误发生在CANN的深层逻辑中，未被上层精准捕获。

- **常见原因**：
  
  1. **算子输入Shape不合法**：例如某个MatMul的K维度不匹配。
  2. **动态Shape编译失败**：模型输入的Shape变化超出了预编译的范围。
  3. **算子实现Bug**：使用了某些冷门算子的特定参数组合，触发了底层Bug。
- **排查手段**：必须将日志级别调至 `INFO` 或 `DEBUG`，重跑程序。在 `ERROR` 出现的前几行，通常会打印出当前正在执行的算子名称（OpName）和参数。拿着这个OpName去搜对应的算子约束文档，往往能发现问题。

#### [](https://blog.csdn.net/qq_46207024/article/details/157128456)[](https://blog.csdn.net/qq_46207024/article/details/157128456)2.2 ACL Stream Synchronize Failed：同步失败

这对应CUDA的 `CUDA Error: Device-side assert triggered`。它表示Host侧在等待Device侧计算完成时，收到了失败信号。

- **常见原因**：
  
  1. **OOM（显存溢出）**：申请显存失败。
  2. **数值计算异常**：FP16计算溢出（NaN/Inf），导致后续算子崩溃。
  3. **AICore Error**：算子内部逻辑错误，比如除以零。
- **排查手段**：
  
  - 检查 `npu-smi info` 监控显存占用。
  - 开启算子溢出检测（Overflow Check）。在PyTorch中，可以使用 `torch_npu.npu.utils.check_overflow(model)`。
  - 尝试开启混合精度训练中的 Loss Scaling，避免梯度下溢。

#### [](https://blog.csdn.net/qq_46207024/article/details/157128456)[](https://blog.csdn.net/qq_46207024/article/details/157128456)2.3 HCCL Error：多卡通信噩梦

在训练DeepSeek-67B等多卡模型时，HCCL（Huawei Collective Communication Library）错误是家常便饭。

- **典型报错**：`HCCL_E_INTERNAL`，`Socket Connection Failed`。
- **常见原因**：
  
  1. **网络配置错误**：`/etc/hccn.conf` 配置不对，或者IP地址冲突。
  2. **防火墙拦截**：多机训练时，节点间的特定端口被防火墙阻断。
  3. **P2P访问受限**：某些服务器架构下，PCIe Switch不支持P2P通信。
  4. **超时（Timeout）**：某张卡计算太慢，导致其他卡在AllReduce时等待超时。
- **排查手段**：
  
  - 使用 `hccn_tool` 检查网卡状态和链路连通性。
  - 调大 `HCCL_EXEC_TIMEOUT` 环境变量（默认是1800秒），排除计算慢导致的误报。
  - 检查物理连线，确认NPU之间的HCCS线缆没有松动。

#### [](https://blog.csdn.net/qq_46207024/article/details/157128456)[](https://blog.csdn.net/qq_46207024/article/details/157128456)2.4 Compile Failed：算子编译失败

如果程序在启动阶段卡住很久，然后报错退出，通常是ATC（Ascend Tensor Compiler）编译算子失败。

- **常见原因**：
  
  1. **算子版本不匹配**：使用的算子参数在当前CANN版本中已被废弃。
  2. **TBE逻辑错误**：自定义算子的Python实现有语法错误。
- **排查手段**：查看 `kernel_meta` 目录下的 `.o` 和 `.json` 文件，确认是哪个算子编译失败。通常日志会给出非常具体的 Python Traceback。

### [](https://blog.csdn.net/qq_46207024/article/details/157128456)[](https://blog.csdn.net/qq_46207024/article/details/157128456)3. 像侦探一样排查：三步走策略

面对海量日志，盲目翻阅是效率最低的。建议遵循“隔离-定位-求助”的三步走策略。

#### [](https://blog.csdn.net/qq_46207024/article/details/157128456)[](https://blog.csdn.net/qq_46207024/article/details/157128456)第一步：隔离变量（Isolation）

确定问题的边界。

- **硬件隔离**：跑一下 `Ascend-cann-toolkit` 自带的 `sample` 样例（如ResNet50）。如果样例都跑不通，说明是驱动、固件或硬件物理故障，不要浪费时间改代码，直接找运维或华为支撑。
- **模型隔离**：如果是微调DeepSeek报错，试着跑一下Llama2或Qwen的脚本。如果其他模型能跑，说明问题出在DeepSeek特定的算子或结构上。
- **算子隔离**：如果怀疑是某个算子问题，尝试用 `torch_npu` 单独编写一个脚本，只运行这一个算子，构造相同的Input Shape，看是否复现。

#### [](https://blog.csdn.net/qq_46207024/article/details/157128456)[](https://blog.csdn.net/qq_46207024/article/details/157128456)第二步：定位算子（Locate Operator）

大模型由成千上万个算子组成，到底是哪一颗螺丝松了？

- **Dump工具**：这是终极核武器。通过配置 `acl.json` 开启算子 Dump 功能，可以将每一层算子的输入（Input）和输出（Output）数据保存到磁盘。
- **比对分析**：将NPU上Dump出的数据，与CPU或GPU上运行同一模型的数据进行逐层比对（Vector Comparison）。通常使用 `msaccucmp.py` 工具。一旦发现某一层的结果误差超过 `1e-3`，那么罪魁祸首就是上一层的算子。

#### [](https://blog.csdn.net/qq_46207024/article/details/157128456)[](https://blog.csdn.net/qq_46207024/article/details/157128456)第三步：利用社区力量

昇腾社区（HiAscend）和 Gitee 是巨大的资源库。

1. **查 Issue**：在 Gitee 的 `Ascend/pytorch` 或 `Ascend/ModelZoo` 仓库 Issue 区，搜索报错关键字。很多共性问题（比如特定CANN版本的Bug）都有人反馈过，并且往往附带了临时规避方案（Workaround）。
2. **提 Issue**：如果确实是新问题，大胆地提 Issue。昇腾的研发团队在 Gitee 上非常活跃。为了获得快速响应，请务必提供“三要素”：
   
   - **环境信息**：`npu-smi info` 截图，CANN版本号（`cat /usr/local/Ascend/ascend-toolkit/latest/version.cfg`）。
   - **复现脚本**：最小化的可复现代码片段。
   - **关键日志**：PLOG 中 ERROR 上下文的 100 行。

### [](https://blog.csdn.net/qq_46207024/article/details/157128456)[](https://blog.csdn.net/qq_46207024/article/details/157128456)4. 那些年我们踩过的坑：实战案例

#### [](https://blog.csdn.net/qq_46207024/article/details/157128456)[](https://blog.csdn.net/qq_46207024/article/details/157128456)案例一：动态Shape导致的内存泄漏

**现象**：DeepSeek推理服务运行几小时后，显存逐渐飙升直至OOM。  
**分析**：查看日志发现大量的 `Compiling op...`。原来是由于输入 Prompt 长度不固定，导致CANN反复编译生成不同Shape的算子Kernel，这些Kernel占用了大量显存且未释放。  
**解决**：在推理脚本中开启“分档”（Bucketing）策略，将输入长度补齐到固定的几个档位（如512, 1024, 2048），命中已编译的Kernel缓存。

#### [](https://blog.csdn.net/qq_46207024/article/details/157128456)[](https://blog.csdn.net/qq_46207024/article/details/157128456)案例二：环境污染引发的符号冲突

**现象**：`ImportError: /usr/lib64/libstdc++.so.6: version 'GLIBCXX_3.4.30' not found`。  
**分析**：这是典型的环境污染。用户在同一个终端里安装了Conda环境，又 source 了昇腾的 `set_env.sh`，导致系统库路径混乱。  
**解决**：坚持“纯净主义”。每次调试新任务，务必新开一个终端，按照标准顺序 source 环境变量。或者将所有环境变量写入 Dockerfile，通过容器隔离环境。

故障排查的过程是痛苦的，但也是成长的快车道。当你能够熟练地从几千行日志中一眼定位到那个 mismatched shape，你就真正掌控了这台算力怪兽。记住，每一个报错代码背后，都是一次深入理解计算机体系结构的机会。

[](https://blog.csdn.net/qq_46207024/article/details/157128456)

https://www.hiascend.com/document/detail/zh/CANNCommunityEdition/900beta2/maintenref/troubleshooting/troubleshooting_0006.html

# Compute Sanitizer — Extracted Reference

## Summary

Compute Sanitizer is NVIDIA's functional correctness checking suite bundled with the CUDA toolkit. It ships four tools — **memcheck** (default), **racecheck**, **initcheck**, and **synccheck** — all invoked through a single `compute-sanitizer` executable. Below is everything relevant to your request, including exact commands, options, error-report formats, compilation flags, and version notes. **Important caveat:** the provided documentation does *not* explicitly mention "device-side assert" anywhere; the closest applicable category is "hardware exception" (details in §5).

---

## 1. What Compute Sanitizer Is

> "Compute Sanitizer is a functional correctness checking suite included in the CUDA toolkit. This suite contains multiple tools that can perform different type of checks."

- **Purpose:** CUDA applications run thousands of threads in parallel; memory access errors and thread-ordering hazards are hard to detect. Compute Sanitizer is designed to detect those problems.
- **How to get it:** "Compute Sanitizer is installed as part of the CUDA toolkit."
- **Compilation requirement:** "The Compute Sanitizer tools do not need any special compilation flags to function" (but see §6 for flags that improve output).

---

## 2. The Four Tools

| Tool | Function | Enable with |
|---|---|---|
| **Memcheck** | "The memory access error and leak detection tool." Precisely detects out-of-bounds and misaligned accesses to global, local, and shared memory; reports hardware exceptions; detects malloc/free errors, CUDA API errors, and memory leaks. | `--tool memcheck` (**default**) |
| **Racecheck** | "The shared memory data access hazard detection tool." Detects WAW, WAR, RAW hazards in on-chip `__shared__` memory. | `--tool racecheck` |
| **Initcheck** | "The uninitialized device global memory access detection tool." Detects reads of device memory never initialized via device writes or `cudaMemcpy`/`cudaMemset`. | `--tool initcheck` |
| **Synccheck** | "The thread synchronization hazard detection tool." Checks correct usage of `__syncthreads()`, `__syncwarp()`, and Cooperative Groups counterparts. | `--tool synccheck` |

⚠️ Note stated for racecheck/initcheck/synccheck: they "do not perform any memory access error checking. It is recommended that users first run the memcheck tool to ensure the application is free of errors."

---

## 3. How to Run It (Command Line Usage)

**General syntax:**

```
compute-sanitizer [options] app_name [app_options]
```

**Per-tool syntax:**

```
compute-sanitizer --tool memcheck [sanitizer_options] app_name [app_options]
compute-sanitizer --tool racecheck [sanitizer_options] app_name [app_options]
compute-sanitizer --tool initcheck [sanitizer_options] app_name [app_options]
compute-sanitizer --tool synccheck [sanitizer_options] app_name [app_options]
```

Since the tool simply takes `app_name [app_options]`, a Python program is run by making `python` the app and the script its argument:

```
compute-sanitizer --tool memcheck python app.py
```

Use `--` to terminate the sanitizer's option list: "All subsequent words are treated as the application being run and its arguments."

**Key general options (from Table 1):**

| Option | Values | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `--tool` | `memcheck, racecheck, initcheck, synccheck` | `memcheck` | Controls which tool is actively running |
| `--destroy-on-device-error` | `context, kernel` | `context` | How the app proceeds on a memory access error |
| `--error-exitcode` | `{number}` | `0` | Exit code returned when the app succeeded but errors were detected (for automated test suites) |
| `--launch-count (c)` | `{number}` | `0` (unlimited) | Limit number of kernel launches checked |
| `--launch-skip (s)` | `{number}` | `0` | Skip N matching kernel launches before checking |
| `--kernel-name` / `--kernel-name-exclude` | `key=value` filters (`kne=`, `kns=`, `regex=`) | N/A | Filter which kernels are checked |
| `--print-limit` | `{number}` | `100` | Stop printing after N errors; 0 = unlimited |
| `--log-file` / `--save` | `{filename}` | N/A | Write output to file (supports escape sequences `%p`, `%q{ENVVAR}`, `%%`) |
| `--force-blocking-launches` | flag | disabled | Serialize kernel launches; reduces number/precision of reported errors |
| `--force-synchronization-limit` | `{number}` | `0` | Force sync after N launches without sync; reduces memory footprint |
| `--generate-coredump` | flag | disabled | Generate CUDA coredump on first error, then stop (**CUDA 11.6+**) |
| `--suppressions` | `{filename}` | N/A | XML file of reports to filter out |

**Backtrace options (Table 2):**

| Option | Values | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `--show-backtrace` | `yes, host, device, no` | `yes` | Backtraces for most error types |
| `--backtrace-short` | `yes, no` | `yes` | Omits CUDART frames and frames below `main()` |

**Notable memcheck options (Table 3):** `--leak-check full` (enables leak detection; default `no`), `--padding {number}` (adds guard buffers after each allocation to catch out-of-bounds into adjacent buffers), `--report-api-errors all|extended|explicit|no` (default `extended`), `--track-stream-ordered-races all|use-before-alloc|use-after-free|no` (default `all`), `--check-device-heap yes|no` (default `yes`).

---

## 4. How Errors Are Reported (Out-of-Bounds / Misaligned, with Kernel Name + Line Number)

Memcheck reports two precision classes:

- **Precise errors** (memory access errors, malloc/free errors, API errors, cudaMalloc leaks): the tool reports **block/thread coordinates, the PC of the offending instruction, the accessed address, its size and type**. Critically:
  > "If the CUDA application contains line number information (by either being compiled with device side debugging information, or with line information), then the tool will also print the source file and line number of the erroneous access."
- **Imprecise errors** (hardware exceptions, device heap leaks): reported by the hardware error mechanism; often cannot be attributed to a specific source line.

**Canonical precise out-of-bounds/misaligned report (verbatim from docs):**

```
========= Invalid __global__ write of size 4 bytes
=========     at unaligned_kernel():0x160 in memcheck_demo.cu:6
=========     by thread (0,0,0) in block (0,0,0)
=========     Address 0x7f6510c00001 is misaligned
```

Line-by-line meaning:
1. `Invalid __global__ write of size 4 bytes` — memory segment (`__global__`, `__shared__`, or `__local__`), access type (read/write), and access size in bytes.
2. `at unaligned_kernel():0x160 in memcheck_demo.cu:6` — **kernel name**, instruction offset (0x160), and — **if compiled with line info** — source file and line number.
3. `by thread (0,0,0) in block (0,0,0)` — `threadIdx` / `blockIdx` of the faulting thread.
4. `Address 0x7f6510c00001 is misaligned` — the accessed address; error type is either **out of bounds** or **misaligned**.

Other report types:
- **Hardware exception:** "Imprecise errors... have a variety of formats and messages. Typically, the first line will provide some information about the type of error encountered."
- **Malloc/free error:** e.g., `========= Malloc/Free error encountered : Double free` (types: Double free, Invalid pointer to free, Heap corruption).
- **Leaks:** `========= Leaked 64 bytes at 0x400200200` (requires `--leak-check full`).
- **CUDA API error:** `========= Program hit invalid copy direction for memcpy (error 21) on CUDA API call to cudaMemcpy.`

**Error actions (Table 9):** Memory access errors, hardware exceptions, malloc/free errors, and synchronization errors default to **Terminate CUDA context** (user can switch memory-access/malloc-free/sync errors to *terminate kernel* via `--destroy-on-device-error`). Leaks, API errors, and shared-memory hazards only report; the application continues.

---

## 5. Device-Side Assert — What the Document Says (and Doesn't)

**The provided documentation contains no explicit mention of "device-side assert" or `assert()`.** The closest applicable error category is:

| Name | Description | Location | Precision |
|---|---|---|---|
| Hardware exception | "Errors that are reported by the hardware error reporting mechanism." | Device | Imprecise |

Practical note (context, not from this document): in practice, a device-side assert failure (`assert()` in kernel code) surfaces under memcheck as a **hardware exception** — i.e., an *imprecise* error, which per this doc "could not be precisely attributed to a particular thread," and memcheck "may not be able to attribute the cause of the error back to the source file and line." Compiling with `-lineinfo`/`-G` and keeping `--show-backtrace yes` (the default) gives the best chance of source-level attribution. Per the Error Actions table, hardware exceptions **terminate the CUDA context** and "subsequent calls on the CUDA context will fail."

---

## 6. Compilation Flags for Line Information

From the **Compilation Options** section:

- **`-lineinfo`** (nvcc): "To generate line number information for applications without affecting the optimization level of the output." — This is the recommended flag for source attribution of errors.
- **`-G`** (nvcc): "forces the compiler to generate debug information for the CUDA application" (full device debug; affects optimization).
- **`-fdevice-sanitize=memcheck`** (nvcc): enables **compile-time instrumentation** for memcheck — "provides significant performance improvements and enhanced error detection through base-and-bounds analysis."
  > ⚠️ "Option `-fdevice-sanitize=memcheck` does not imply the generation of debug information. To get source attribution of errors, the application must also be compiled with `-lineinfo` or `-G`."
- **Host backtrace symbols:** on Linux, host compiler needs `-rdynamic`; on Windows, `/Zi` (compile for debugging). Pass via nvcc's `-Xcompiler`.
- **Device backtrace:** full frame info requires device debug info; "the compiler can skip generation of frame information when building with optimizations."

**Sample build commands (verbatim):**

```
nvcc -Xcompiler -rdynamic -lineinfo  -o out in.cu
```

```
nvcc -fdevice-sanitize=memcheck -lineinfo -o out in.cu
```

Compile-time patching can also be enabled directly via ptxas:

```
ptxas -arch=sm_75 -sanitize=memcheck myapp.ptx
```

Requirement: "The PTX compiler and compute-sanitizer must be from the same CUDA toolkit version for compile-time patching." Hybrid instrumentation is supported — uninstrumented compilation units fall back to runtime binary instrumentation.

---

## 7. Version Notes Preserved

- **CUDA 11.6+:** `--generate-coredump` coredump support; OptiX 7 support with memcheck and initcheck (`--check-optix`). Coredump restrictions: only erroring threads inspectable; racecheck not supported.
- **CUDA 11.7+:** OptiX resource leak detection (`--check-optix-leaks` for `OptixModule`, `optixPipeline`, `optixProgramGroup`, `optixDenoiser`); supported for OptiX API 7.0–9.0.
- **Ampere and newer:** `cuda::barrier` support in racecheck/synccheck; async copy (`cuda::memcpy_async` / PTX `cp.async`, compute capability 8.0) race detection via `--racecheck-memcpy-async`.
- **Hopper (sm_90a):** `--check-warpgroup-mma` (default yes) for PTX `wgmma` instruction checks in memcheck and synccheck; `--check-bulk-copy` for `cp.async.bulk`.
- **Blackwell:** TCMMA guardrails for `tcgen05.*` instructions via ptxas flag `-g-tmem-access-check`; invalid TCMMA ops reported by memcheck.
- **Compile-time patching:** currently memcheck-only; racecheck, initcheck, synccheck still require runtime binary instrumentation.
- **Platforms:** Windows requires registry key `HKEY_LOCAL_MACHINE\SOFTWARE\NVIDIA Corporation\GPUDebugger\EnableInterface = 1`; TDR timeout (`TdrDelay`) may need raising since sanitizer slows kernels. Jetson/Tegra: user must be in the `debug` group (`sudo usermod -a -G debug $USER`).
- **CUDA Fortran:** use `--language fortran` for 1-based thread/block indices.

---

## Quick-Start Recipe (as requested)

```bash
# Build with line info so errors include kernel name + file:line
nvcc -Xcompiler -rdynamic -lineinfo -o out in.cu

# Or with faster compile-time memcheck instrumentation
nvcc -fdevice-sanitize=memcheck -lineinfo -o out in.cu

# Run (native binary)
compute-sanitizer --tool memcheck ./out

# Run (Python script — app_name is python, script is its argument)
compute-sanitizer --tool memcheck python app.py

# Useful extras
compute-sanitizer --tool memcheck --leak-check full --show-backtrace yes \
    --destroy-on-device-error kernel --error-exitcode 1 python app.py
```

**Expected precise error output** (with `-lineinfo`): kernel name, instruction offset, source file:line, thread/block indices, address, and whether it's out-of-bounds or misaligned. Device-side assert failures, while not named in this doc, fall under *imprecise hardware exceptions* and terminate the CUDA context by default.

https://pytorch.org/docs/stable/notes/cuda.html

# Analysis: CUDA Error Debugging Content in PyTorch CUDA Semantics Documentation

## Summary of Findings

I analyzed the provided PyTorch 2.14 "CUDA semantics" documentation for debugging-related content. The document contains **limited but relevant** material on this topic. Specifically:

- ✅ **`CUDA_LAUNCH_BLOCKING`** — Covered (in the Asynchronous execution section)
- ✅ **Asynchronous CUDA kernel error reporting** — Covered (explains *why* errors are hard to trace)
- ❌ **`TORCH_USE_CUDA_DSA` (device-side assertions)** — **Not mentioned** in this document
- ❌ **"Device-side assert triggered" errors** — **Not explicitly discussed**
- ⚠️ **Locating the failing kernel/line** — Only indirectly addressed via `CUDA_LAUNCH_BLOCKING` and a `cuda-memcheck` tip

---

## 1. `CUDA_LAUNCH_BLOCKING` Environment Variable

The exact guidance from the **Asynchronous execution** section:

> "You can force synchronous computation by setting environment variable `CUDA_LAUNCH_BLOCKING=1`. This can be handy when an error occurs on the GPU. (With asynchronous execution, such an error isn't reported until after the operation is actually executed, so the stack trace does not show where it was requested.)"

**Key points:**
- **Exact variable name:** `CUDA_LAUNCH_BLOCKING=1`
- **Purpose:** Forces synchronous computation so that GPU errors are reported at the point where the operation was *requested*, rather than when it was actually *executed* — making the stack trace point to the true source of the error.

---

## 2. Asynchronous Error Reporting (Why Errors Are Hard to Localize)

The documentation explains the root cause of confusing CUDA error locations:

> "By default, GPU operations are asynchronous. When you call a function that uses the GPU, the operations are *enqueued* to the particular device, but not necessarily executed until later."

> "In general, the effect of asynchronous computation is invisible to the caller, because (1) each device executes operations in the order they are queued, and (2) PyTorch automatically performs necessary synchronization when copying data between CPU and GPU or between two GPUs. Hence, computation will proceed as if every operation was executed synchronously."

**Implication for debugging:** Because kernels execute asynchronously, a runtime error (e.g., an illegal memory access) surfaces at a *later, unrelated* API call — which is precisely the scenario `CUDA_LAUNCH_BLOCKING=1` is designed to address.

---

## 3. Related Debugging Tip: Memory Error Checking

From the **Memory management** section, a related debugging recommendation:

> "Use of a caching allocator can interfere with memory checking tools such as `cuda-memcheck`. To debug memory errors using `cuda-memcheck`, set `PYTORCH_NO_CUDA_MEMORY_CACHING=1` in your environment to disable caching."

- **Exact variable name:** `PYTORCH_NO_CUDA_MEMORY_CACHING=1`
- **Use case:** Debugging memory errors with NVIDIA's `cuda-memcheck` tool, which conflicts with PyTorch's caching allocator.

---

## 4. Content NOT Found in This Document

The following requested topics are **absent** from the provided page:

| Requested Topic | Status |
|---|---|
| `TORCH_USE_CUDA_DSA` | ❌ Not mentioned anywhere in the document |
| Device-side assertions / "device-side assert triggered" | ❌ Not discussed |
| Compile flags for device-side assert support | ❌ Not covered |
| Kernel-level error localization (beyond `CUDA_LAUNCH_BLOCKING`) | ❌ Not covered |

For those topics, you would need to consult other PyTorch documentation sources (e.g., build-from-source docs, troubleshooting guides, or release notes), as this "CUDA semantics" page focuses on execution semantics, memory management, streams, and CUDA Graphs rather than compile-time debugging options.

**标题**: CUDA Programming Guide — CUDA Programming Guide

Back to top

# CUDA Programming Guide[#](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-programming-guide/index.html#cuda-programming-guide "Link to this heading")

**CUDA and the CUDA Programming Guide**

CUDA is a parallel computing platform and programming model developed by NVIDIA that enables dramatic increases in computing performance by harnessing the power of the GPU. It allows developers to accelerate compute-intensive applications and is widely used in fields such as deep learning, scientific computing, and high-performance computing (HPC).

This CUDA Programming Guide is the official, comprehensive resource on the CUDA programming model and how to write code that executes on the GPU using the CUDA platform. This guide covers everything from the CUDA programming model and the CUDA platform to the details of language extensions and covers how to make use of specific hardware and software features. This guide provides a pathway for developers to learn CUDA if they are new, and also provides an essential resource for developers as they build applications using CUDA.

**Organization of This Guide**

Even for developers who primarily use libraries, frameworks, or DSLs, an understanding of the CUDA programming model and how GPUs execute code is valuable in knowing what is happening behind the layers of abstraction. This guide starts with a chapter on the [CUDA programming model](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-programming-guide/01-introduction/programming-model.html#programming-model) outside of any specific programming language which is applicable to anyone interested in understanding how CUDA works, even non-developers.

The guide is broken down into five primary parts:

- Part 1: Introduction and Programming Model Abstract
  
  - A language agnostic overview of the CUDA programming model as well as a brief tour of the CUDA platform.
  - This section is meant to be read by anyone wanting to understand GPUs and the concepts of executing code on GPUs, even if they are not developers.
- Part 2: Programming GPUs in CUDA
  
  - The basics of programming GPUs in C++ and Python.
  - This section is meant to be read by anyone wanting to get started in GPU programming.
  - This section is meant to be instructional, not complete, and teaches the most important and common parts of CUDA programming, including some common performance considerations.
- Part 3: Advanced CUDA
  
  - Introduces some more advance features of CUDA that enable both fine-grained control and more opportunities to maximize performance, including the use of multiple GPUs in a single application.
  - This section concludes with a [tour of the features covered in part 4](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-programming-guide/03-advanced/feature-survey.html#tour-of-features) with a brief introduction to the purpose and function of each, sorted by when and why a developer may find each feature useful.
- Part 4: CUDA Features
  
  - This section contains complete coverage of specific CUDA features such as CUDA graphs, dynamic parallelism, interoperability with graphics APIs, and unified memory.
  - This section should be consulted when knowing the complete picture of a specific CUDA feature is needed. Where possible, care has been taken to introduce and motivate the features covered in this section in earlier sections.
- Part 5: Technical Appendices
  
  - The technical appendices provide some reference documentation on CUDA’s C++ high-level language support, hardware-specific specifications, and other technical specifications.
  - This section is meant as technical reference for specific description of syntax, semantics, and technical behavior of elements of CUDA.

Parts 1-3 provide a guided learning experience for developers new to CUDA, though they also provide insight and updated information useful for CUDA developers of any experience level.

Parts 4 and 5 provide a wealth of information about specific features and detailed topics, and are intended to provide a curated, well-organized reference for developers needing to know more details as they write CUDA applications.

# 【已解决】RuntimeError: CUDA error: device-side assert triggeredCUDA kernel errors might be asynchronous 原创

> 😎 作者介绍：我是程序员行者孙，一个热爱分享技术的制能工人。计算机本硕，人工制能研究生。公众号：AI Sun，视频号：AI-行者Sun

## [](https://blog.csdn.net/festaw/article/details/138765801)[](https://blog.csdn.net/festaw/article/details/138765801)问题

**报错RuntimeError: CUDA error: device-side assert triggeredCUDA kernel errors might be asynchronous**  
![在这里插入图片描述](https://i-blog.csdnimg.cn/blog_migrate/4a873202553bfccec914873bd2d3d947.png)

## [](https://blog.csdn.net/festaw/article/details/138765801)[](https://blog.csdn.net/festaw/article/details/138765801)报错原因分析

完整报错：  
RuntimeError: CUDA error: device-side assert triggered  
CUDA kernel errors might be asynchronously reported at some other API call, so the stacktrace below might be incorrect.  
For debugging consider passing CUDA_LAUNCH_BLOCKING=1.  
Compile with `TORCH_USE_CUDA_DSA` to enable device-side assertions.

我们翻译一下：

1. **RuntimeError: CUDA error: device-side assert triggered**
   
   - 运行时错误：CUDA设备端断言被触发。
   - 这表明在GPU设备上执行的代码中有断言失败。断言是一种错误检查，如果代码中的某个条件为假，则会引发断言失败。
2. **CUDA kernel errors might be asynchronously reported at some other API call, so the stacktrace below might be incorrect.**
   
   - CUDA核心（kernel）错误可能会在某些其他API调用中异步报告，因此下面的堆栈跟踪可能不正确。
   - 由于CUDA操作可能是异步执行的，错误可能不会立即在引发它的确切位置被报告。这意味着错误消息中的堆栈跟踪可能不指向实际引发问题的位置。
3. **For debugging consider passing CUDA_LAUNCH_BLOCKING=1.**
   
   - 为了调试，请考虑传递CUDA_LAUNCH_BLOCKING=1。
   - 这是一个调试技巧。通过设置环境变量CUDA_LAUNCH_BLOCKING=1，可以使CUDA操作同步执行，这有助于准确地定位引发错误的代码行。
4. **Compile with ‘TORCH_USE_CUDA_DSA’ to enable device-side assertions.**
   
   - 使用‘TORCH_USE_CUDA_DSA’编译以启用设备端断言。
   - 这建议在编译PyTorch时启用设备端断言，这可以帮助开发者捕捉到在GPU上执行的代码中的问题。

## [](https://blog.csdn.net/festaw/article/details/138765801)[](https://blog.csdn.net/festaw/article/details/138765801)解决方法

1. **在线搜索解决方案**：
   
   - 我首先在网上搜索了这个问题的解决方案，发现许多网友建议错误可能是由于分类任务中训练数据标签超出了设定的类别数目所导致的。例如，如果设置了8个类别，但训练数据中的标签却包含了9，就会触发这个错误。此外，还需要注意一个常见的陷阱：即使标签从0开始计数，在PyTorch中使用0或负数作为类别标签也会导致错误。因此，如果类别标签从0开始，需要将所有类别标签加1以避免错误。
   - 根据PyTorch的工作原理，它会扫描存放训练数据的`train_path`目录下每个文件夹（每个类别的图片都存放在自己的文件夹中），并将每个类别映射为一个数值。例如，如果有4个类别，类别标签将被映射为[0, 1, 2, 3]。在二分类问题中，标签会被映射为[0, 1]，但在多分类问题中，标签的映射可能会不同，这有时会导致错误。
2. **代码审查**：
   
   - 尽管我按照上述思路进行了修改，但问题依然存在。因此，我仔细检查了代码，最终发现问题并不在于标签与类别数目不匹配，而在于神经网络最后一层的设置有误。具体来说，输出层的设置需要与模型应该输出的类别数目一致。
   - 在我的模型中，原本的代码是这样的：
     
     ```python
     self.outlayer = nn.Linear(256 * 1 * 1, 3)  # 这是一个3分类问题的全连接层设置
     
     11
     ```
   - 但由于我面对的是5分类问题，我需要修改全连接层以反映正确的类别数目，修改后的代码如下：
     
     ```python
     self.outlayer = nn.Linear(256 * 1 * 1, 5)  # 修改为5分类问题的全连接层设置
     
     11
     ```
   - 经过这次修改，错误得到了解决。

总结来说，解决这个问题需要仔细检查数据的标签处理以及神经网络模型的设置，确保它们与问题的实际需求相匹配。在调试过程中，对代码的每一个细节都进行仔细的审查是非常关键的。

祝大家实验顺利，有效涨点~

# 解决PyTorch中的RuntimeError: CUDA error: device-side assert triggered

### 🔍 解决PyTorch中的RuntimeError: CUDA error: device-side assert triggered

#### 摘要

大家好，我是默语，擅长全栈开发、运维和[人工智能](https://cloud.tencent.com/developer/techpedia/1500?from_column=20065&from=20065)技术。在这篇文章中，我将深入探讨如何解决PyTorch中的一个常见错误：RuntimeError: CUDA error: device-side assert triggered。这个错误常常让开发者头疼，因为它涉及到GPU设备的断言错误，可能会导致程序崩溃。通过本文的详细分析和代码示例，希望帮助大家顺利解决这个问题，提高模型的运行效率。

#### 引言

在深度学习模型的训练过程中，使用GPU加速计算是非常常见的。然而，由于GPU的复杂性，时常会遇到一些难以调试的问题。PyTorch作为一个灵活且强大的[深度学习框架](https://cloud.tencent.com/developer/techpedia/1757?from_column=20065&from=20065)，也不例外。在使用PyTorch进行深度学习训练时，`RuntimeError: CUDA error: device-side assert triggered`是一个比较常见的错误。这个错误通常是由于GPU设备在执行过程中发生断言失败，引发了程序崩溃。接下来，我们将详细介绍这个错误的产生原因及其解决方案。

#### 正文内容（详细介绍）

##### 1. 错误产生的原因

###### 1.1 数据类型不匹配

在PyTorch中，张量的数据类型必须与模型的预期类型匹配。如果存在不匹配，可能会导致CUDA设备上的断言失败。例如，如果模型期望输入是`torch.FloatTensor`类型，而实际输入是`torch.LongTensor`，就会引发该错误。

###### 1.2 索引超出范围

在使用索引操作时，如果索引超出了张量的范围，也会触发CUDA设备上的断言错误。比如，在进行分类任务时，如果目标标签的索引值超出了类别数的范围，就会导致该错误。

###### 1.3 未正确初始化的张量

在某些情况下，未正确初始化的张量也会导致设备断言错误。例如，在使用未初始化的张量进行计算时，可能会触发此错误。

##### 2. 解决方案

###### 2.1 检查数据类型

确保所有输入张量的数据类型与模型预期的类型一致。在代码中可以使用`.float()`或`.long()`方法进行类型转换。

代码语言：javascript

AI代码解释

复制

```javascript
# 示例代码：将输入张量转换为浮点类型
inputs = inputs.float()
```

###### 2.2 验证索引范围

在进行索引操作之前，确保索引值在张量的有效范围内。对于分类任务，确保目标标签的索引值在类别数的范围内。

代码语言：javascript

AI代码解释

复制

```javascript
# 示例代码：验证索引范围
num_classes = 10
labels = torch.randint(0, num_classes, (batch_size,))
```

###### 2.3 初始化张量

在使用张量之前，确保所有张量已正确初始化。

代码语言：javascript

AI代码解释

复制

```javascript
# 示例代码：正确初始化张量
tensor = torch.zeros((batch_size, num_features), dtype=torch.float32)
```

##### 3. 代码示例

以下是一个完整的示例代码，演示了如何处理和解决`RuntimeError: CUDA error: device-side assert triggered`错误。

代码语言：javascript

AI代码解释

复制

```javascript
import torch
import torch.nn as nn
import torch.optim as optim

# 定义简单的模型
class SimpleModel(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, num_features, num_classes):
        super(SimpleModel, self).__init__()
        self.fc = nn.Linear(num_features, num_classes)

    def forward(self, x):
        return self.fc(x)

# 初始化模型和优化器
model = SimpleModel(num_features=100, num_classes=10)
optimizer = optim.SGD(model.parameters(), lr=0.01)

# 生成示例数据
inputs = torch.randn(32, 100)  # batch_size=32, num_features=100
labels = torch.randint(0, 10, (32,))  # num_classes=10

# 将数据移到GPU
inputs = inputs.cuda()
labels = labels.cuda()
model = model.cuda()

# 将输入转换为浮点类型
inputs = inputs.float()

# 前向传播
outputs = model(inputs)

# 计算损失
criterion = nn.CrossEntropyLoss()
loss = criterion(outputs, labels)

# 反向传播和优化
loss.backward()
optimizer.step()
```

##### 🤔 QA环节

###### Q1: 为什么会出现`RuntimeError: CUDA error: device-side assert triggered`错误？

A1: 这个错误通常是由于数据类型不匹配、索引超出范围或未正确初始化张量引起的。详细原因见上文。

###### Q2: 如何快速定位错误源？

A2: 可以通过逐步检查数据类型、验证索引范围以及确保张量初始化来快速定位错误源。结合CUDA的调试工具也可以更高效地找到问题。

#### 小结

在本文中，我们详细探讨了`RuntimeError: CUDA error: device-side assert triggered`错误的产生原因及其解决方案。通过检查数据类型、验证索引范围和确保张量初始化，可以有效地解决这一错误。希望本文能够帮助大家在使用PyTorch时更加顺利地解决类似问题，提高模型的训练效率。

## Supplementary Reference (General Knowledge — NOT Verbatim From the Provided Excerpt)

To still be helpful, below is the substance of those sections from general knowledge of this document. **This text is not copied from the provided content and should be verified against the live page** (https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-c-programming-guide/index.html):

### 3.2.12 Error Checking (typical content)

- All runtime functions return an error code; for asynchronous functions, it only reports host-side errors (e.g., parameter validation), not asynchronous device errors.
- To catch asynchronous errors, synchronize (e.g., `cudaDeviceSynchronize()`) and check its return code.
- The runtime maintains a per-host-thread error variable initialized to `cudaSuccess`.
  - `cudaPeekAtLastError()` — returns the last error **without resetting** the variable.
  - `cudaGetLastError()` — returns the last error **and resets** it to `cudaSuccess`.
- Kernel launches return no error code, so `cudaGetLastError()` should be called immediately after launch for pre-launch errors, followed by synchronization for asynchronous errors.
- `cudaErrorAssert` (reported as **"device-side assert triggered"**) is returned when a device-side assertion fails.
- `cudaErrorNotReady` (from `cudaStreamQuery()`/`cudaEventQuery()`) is **not** considered an error and is not reported.

### 7.31 Assertion (typical content)

- `assert()` is supported in device code.
- On assertion failure, the kernel **traps**; execution is aborted and the host receives `cudaErrorAssert` ("device-side assert triggered").
- Assertions are enabled in **debug mode** (compiling with `-G` / debug builds) and **disabled when `NDEBUG` is defined** (standard release behavior).

### CUDA_LAUNCH_BLOCKING (from Section 18, Environment Variables)

- `CUDA_LAUNCH_BLOCKING=1` forces kernel launches to execute **synchronously** (disabling normal asynchronous launch behavior), useful for debugging to localize errors.
